- **3. One undo.** `toState({ find, query })` for kids.shop (`{ money, purchases }`) and kids.arith (`{ holdings }`) next to kids.tasks'; the host writes it into the session state after every library change (tell, answer, rewind), created with the world's first answer like ops' lazy state. `ReasonerSession`: `beginTurn` takes `world.mark()`, `commitTurn` keeps it when `world.stamp` moved, `rewind(mark, session)` = library `rewind` + retract the dialogue wires the library no longer holds + state/mirrors derived again, returning a function that puts it back. "undo that", the dialogue layer's `undoAction` ("that's wrong" + "yes") and `_repair` ("no, I meant ...", gives up with the returned function) all call it (`act.rsMark`); without the matrix reasoner the saved state is restored as before. `dlg.kidsB#028#4`, `kidsB#032#1`, `#3` pass. Tests in `tests/reasoner.test.mjs`: shop (revise then "that's wrong"/"yes"), tasks, arith ("undo that"), the redo function, and library = session state = memory in each. Not done: a plain statement ("I buy a ball") is not an action of the dialogue layer (no frame), so "that's wrong" after it does not offer an undo, as in ops; "undo that" takes it back. I tried making it one and it changed "what did you do?" (dlg.kidsB#042#3), so it is reverted.

The eval classifier counts a state key that appears as a change: with `ops`, kids.arith's lazily created empty state appears on the turn ("wrong"); in `matrix` mode it did not, so the turn was "not understood". The host now creates the state of a world with its first answer, as ops does. Test: the same messages on games.guess (and the Romanian line) give identical replies, statuses and state under both settings. Three cases still differ in `eval-reasoner` (kids.shop#043.ro and .roc, dlg.kidsA#010#1): not understood in ops, wrong in matrix, because the matrix keeps shop/arith state and ops keeps none; no reply differs.

## SOPWORLD-1 item 10: One SOP Lang (2026-11-11, worker agent)
- **Spec.** `specs/01-soplang.md` is now SOP Lang v1.1 from the library's `language.md` (0.6.1): `?name` variables (a fourth value kind `{ kind: 'var', v }`; `$?x` is a ref with `v: '?x'`), `#` comments, identifier segments after the first may start with a digit, atoms may contain `= @ $ # ?` (and start with `@`/`#` after `=`), strings may hold raw line ends and tabs, named errors for text after a string, a quote or backslash in an atom, and invalid variables; canonical form unchanged. Kept on purpose: our v1 error wording, no `lenient` mode, and a value directly followed by `@` still starts a declaration.
- **Parser.** `src/soplang/index.mjs` parses v1.1 (comments skipped where a token could start, all new loops tick the budget, resync only at an `@` that starts a token). Facts with variables: the legend validator says `a variable (?x) is only legal in a rule file, not in a fact` (before, `?x` was an atom). Tests: `tests/soplang-v11.test.mjs` parses every `worlds/**/*.sop` with the frozen v1 parser (`tests/fixtures/soplang-v1-reference.mjs`) and the new one: 100+ files identical, the only files v1 rejected are `matrix.sop` and `mapping.sop` (comments) and they parse now. Two old tests changed because v1.1 allows what v1 rejected (`k=a=b`, a raw line end in a string); `tests/matcher.test.mjs` had one emit template gluing `x@{_1}`, now separated by `\n`.
- **One parser.** `src/reasoner/index.mjs` reads every `matrix.sop` with our parser and hands the library wires (`libWire`: `{ id, type, attrs: { key: [writeValue(value)] } }`); legends, kb and mapping still go to the library as text (mapping has comments but is the library's own mapping file; moving them is the same few lines). Parse errors are reported as problems with line and column. kids.shop 147/147, kids.arith 217/217, kids.tasks 232/232 under `--reasoner matrix`.
- **Editor / debug view.** `colorSop` (`docs/webchat/ui/trace.mjs`) colours `?name` / `$?name` (`sop-var`) and comments only where a token starts (`colour=#fff` stays plain); CSS in `style.css`; `docs/soplang.html` bullet updated.

### PERF: turn-time cost of matrix mode (2026-11-11)
- New `scripts/bench-reasoner.mjs` (both engines, same inputs, warmed up, interleaved, p50/p95/mean per world group, `--instrument` for per-method/library-call time per group); results `training/results/reasoner-bench-2026-11-11.json`; section "Turn-time cost of matrix mode" in sop_world_status.md.
- Cause of the 8-15% in non-matrix worlds: `ReasonerSession.tell` ran `world.complete({ defaults: true })` after every `session.add` (also for answers, questions, wires of non-matrix worlds) and every forked session (dry runs, validation) completed again; 78% of all completings were for non-matrix worlds, ~0.17 ms each, several per turn. Fixed in `src/reasoner/index.mjs`: completing is skipped while the library stamp is the one after the last completing (`completedAt`, copied by `fork`), and `_state` is memoized per stamp. Non-matrix worlds now 1.00-1.03x p50 (noise), the four matrix worlds 1.21x -> 1.12x p50. Replies, statuses and state of 1,799 sessions byte-identical in matrix mode before and after (4 random-output sessions aside); npm test (1256 pass), test-world all, matrix worlds, build-webchat pass.
- Not found slow: beginTurn (0.01 ms), commitTurn, refuseFacts, syncTo/mirroring (17 ms in total over 500 cases), explain, rewind, `check` per segment (0.3 ms, 84 calls), `stats` (charge), the world loading (once per engine).
- **Library issue for the sopworld session (do not patch src/vendor).** Function: `World.fork()` / `World.mark()` followed by the first change (`_detach`, sopworld.mjs ~2456). Input: our shared base has 60 wires, 573 matrix entries, 334 index sets, 11 byType sets. Timing (node 22, this machine): `base.fork()` + `tell([core.agent])` 0.116 ms, of which `fork()` + `_detach()` alone 0.070 ms; the same `tell` on an already detached world 0.006 ms; `mark()` + `tell` 0.162 ms. We do mark/fork twice per turn (turn mark, segment mark) and once per dry run, so ~0.1-0.2 ms per turn that changes the library, about a tenth of a matrix-world turn. Cause: `_detach` copies every Map/Set (`sets(this.index)`, `byType`, `refs`, `byWire`, `byRule`, `matrix`, `alias`, `used`, `firings`, `_logOf`, `log`, `events`) even when the change touches one wire. Suggestions: copy-on-write per structure (the matrix, alias and rule indexes do not change on a `tell` of a fact: copy them only when a rule or matrix wire is loaded); share the immutable matrix between forks; or make `mark()` cheap and journal-based (undo log) so that a tell after a mark copies nothing. Also `complete({ defaults: true })` on a world with nothing to fill costs ~0.17 ms (a transaction begin plus `_next()` over the matrix): a fast exit when no wire changed since the last `complete` would make our stamp guard unnecessary.
### PERF round 2 + A/B refresh (2026-11-11, worker agent)
- **Why the measurements disagreed.** `bench-reasoner` uses the operational set (mostly one-turn sessions); `ab-sopworld --sets coherence,dialogues,gold` is multi-turn: statements tell facts in 3-4 dry-run forks plus the real session, each followed by `complete({defaults})` (~1.1 ms), and the game actions run in two dry runs (spelling stage and name repair, same reading) plus the real run. Hangman/guess "new game" is not the cause of the p50 (it is a few turns); guess `act` costs 2-7 ms per call (fork first-change `_detach`, ~70 animal tells).
- **Fixed (outputs byte-identical, 4,733 sessions compared before/after in matrix mode; only random-output sessions differ):** `_mayFill` skips completings that cannot fill anything (`src/reasoner/index.mjs`); `_dryStatuses` shares one dry run per reading per turn (`src/dialog/index.mjs`, both reasoners); `host.view` caches key resolution. p50 gap: four worlds +4.8 -> +2.0 ms, games +4.5 -> +2.6 ms. Table in sop_world_status.md "Second round".
- **A/B refresh:** 6,806 sessions, 20 differ, 0 unjudged: 6 matrix better, 0 ops better, 14 neutral. dlg.kidsB#016 and coh.shop.12 reclassified neutral with reasons (the wording fixes hold). Classes file updated; result .json/.md regenerated (classes applied to the run's output, not rerun).
- **Library issues (for the sopworld session; not patched).** (1) `World.complete({defaults:true})` costs ~1 ms in a world of ~300 wires even when nothing can be filled: `_next()` calls `assumptions()` (a pass over every cell of every wire, `_rests`) on each loop iteration before looking at fills. Suggest: compute fills first and `assumptions()` only when `review` is wanted, or a `complete({ review: false })` / cheap `canComplete()`; we now test it ourselves through `world._compiled().slots` (a private method: please expose a public equivalent). (2) `_detach` on the first change of a fork/after a mark copies every index (~0.5 ms with the animals and kb; grows with base size). (3) `act` of a game action = begin transaction on a shared world => detach, then each `ctx.tell` re-propagates; a `tell` of 70 wires costs ~4 ms in a fresh fork.
- Not done: moving the guess animals into the base (they are told per game); needs a world redesign, not a byte-identical change.

The fact wires carry `said`, `asked`, `req`; the operations record them in a non-enumerable property of the state (`provOf`), so ops state and ops replies are unchanged. English and Romanian ("De ce?" -> "Am notat Cluj la „oraș sau țară”, pentru că mi-ai spus asta. Am aflat asta din ce mi-ai spus: "Locuiesc în Cluj"."). Gap: the user's words in an interview answer are the English reading (the host gives the interview no source id); an interview answer's explanation stays English in a Romanian session because the interview questions are not translated. Follow-up for the host: pass the source id of the message to `interceptInput`, then `req` can be set there too and the quote is as typed.
- **kids.animals: nothing moved; the library cannot express it today.** Searched docs and source: value-mode queries on a key with several values are `conflicting`; `mode=list` (S50) is the only way to get several values as `supported`; `card=many` has no effect on a query; aggregates (`collect`) produce several values again, no aggregate joins them. Our question wires (patterns) are shared with ops and carry no mode; a template without `mode` renders only value questions, so adding `mode=list` to the shared patterns would break the ops templates. Soft constraints already model the "conflicting taught facts are kept and reported" case natively (class, legs, can_fly, can_swim). **Library issue (for the sopworld session):** (1) *Repro:* wires `@a1 animal name=dog habitat=home habitat=farm`, `core.slot of=animal key=habitat many=true`; `world.query({ type: 'animal', seek: 'habitat', where: { name: 'dog' } })` returns `{ status: 'conflicting', alternatives: [home, farm] }`; *expected:* a key declared `many=true` (or `answer=list` on its slot) answers its values as `supported` with `values` even in value mode, as `mode=list` does. (2) *Repro:* a rule `animal name=?n class=?c -> core.on=$a is_a=?c`; `query({ seek: 'is_a', where: { name: 'whale', is_a: 'fish' } })` answers `supported fish` (the supposed wire's own value); *expected:* a way to ask "does the derived value equal X" that answers yes/no/the actual value, for example `mode=check` that compares the `where` value with the derived ones instead of supposing it. With either, lives/covered/is_a move to `matrix.sop` and ops.mjs loses their code.
- **kids.time: skipped, nothing to word.** Every default of the world sits in the operations left to ops (the real clock, the chain of length facts) and keeps its words; no rule of `matrix.sop` rests on a default, so there is no assumption to say. Revisit if the clock or the chain moves into rules.

## 2026-11-11 - docs.editor and docs.reader on the matrix (wave 1 of "every world moves to sopworld")
- Two commits (spec 15, "docs.editor and docs.reader on the matrix"). The text engines stay in code; the state and its validation moved: `adopt`/`toState` like user.profile (the operations keep running on the session state, the world's own instance is the record), plus four generic host additions, all optional exports/keys: `guard` (the rules say, before the operation runs, whether the target exists: "There is no third paragraph; the document has two.", "That section is already deleted.", "There is no sentence 99; the document has 66 sentences."), `redo=` + n-step undo + `isView` (a selection is wires, so without it "undo" would first undo the selection), `stateKey` (cheap change detection instead of stringifying a big state) and `untold=` (fact types that stay in the session memory).
- ops mode is unchanged (scripted-session parity tests; `test-world all` both modes; every existing test passes in the default matrix mode, among them the editor's "50 snapshots" and "history length" tests: the tab and the tests count `history`/`future` entries, so `toState` keeps a count (`histN`) and the host passes the number of redos it holds).
- Tests: `tests/reasoner-docs.test.mjs` (15 tests: wires, derived counts, the denies, invariants, undo/redo words, ops/matrix parity of scripted sessions); Chrome scenarios `editor`, `reader`. Numbers and the per-world row: sop_world_status.md.
- Things that bit: (1) a rule's `when=$p` with a counted pattern fires once per match, so every count was 1: the aggregate needs an anchor wire (the document wire) and then there is no count for an empty document without the anchor; (2) a wire whose keys change cannot be `set` in place, so it is retracted and told under a new id; the first version did not map `x.r1` back to `x` and told duplicates; (3) the Documents tab sets `visible` and `inputMode` when it is shown: adopting that polluted `turns[].worlds` and the generic "undo that" lost its world wording (kids.animals, Chrome); the key now ignores them; (4) "undo" while dictating was taken by the modal input: matrix mode does not offer it (spec 15).
- Not done: `guard` covers kind + number and `chapter N` only (the engine resolves everything else); the reader's `summary`/`stats` section arguments are not guarded; the editor's `history` is a count, not entries; persisted-session restore of a reader/editor with wires was not exercised beyond the tests.

### Library improvements (for the library session, from docs.editor and docs.reader)
1. **Opaque values.** The engine's big immutable analysis (28 kB for a 685-word text, megabytes for a book) must sit in a wire so it is part of marks/rewind, but it is parsed, escaped twice and compared as a string. Idea: a slot `kind=blob` (value kept as given, compared by length+hash, never tokenized, excluded from rule matching). Minimal: `@doc.k core.slot of=docs.reader.open key=json kind=blob`.
2. **Bound variables in the refusal.** `check()` returns `why` with variables already replaced as text, so we post-process the string to turn "has 2" into "has two" and `?n` into "third". Idea: `violations[i].vars = { n: 3, c: 2, o: 'third' }` and filters in the template (`?c:words`, `?n:ordinal`).
3. **Aggregates without an anchor.** `core.calc fn=count over=$p` in a rule needs a separate `when=` wire or it fires once per match; and a count of an empty set has no wire unless an anchor exists. Idea: `core.aggregate over=$p count=?c default=0 then=...` that always yields one result. Minimal: five copies of the same rule today (one per block kind in docs.editor).
4. **Rank of an item.** "The 2nd chapter" is the position of a wire among the wires that match a pattern, ordered by a key. We compute it in JS (`sec`) and write it as an attribute. Idea: `core.rank of=$p by=n out=?r`.
5. **Transient wires.** The selection, the cursor and the focus are state the user looks at, not edits; "undo" must skip a step that changed only them. We do it in the host by comparing states (`isView`). Idea: a type flag `transient=true` (or `core.transient`) so `mark`/`rewind` groups are skipped by `undo` natively and a redo is a first-class operation (`world.redo()`; today `rewind` returns the function).
6. **Upsert.** `applyPatch.sets` changes a single value in place, but adding or removing a key means retract + tell under a new id (`x.r1`), and the host has to map ids back. Idea: `applyPatch({ replace: { id, attrs } })` keeping the id.
7. **String checks.** `core.check` has no `matches` / `startsWith` / `lower`; "a heading that says Chapter 3 is chapter 3" and "the section called Plans" had to stay in the guard's JS.
8. **A dry run with hypothetical facts.** `guard` asks `check({ wires })` with the question as a wire; for an instance that has no wires yet (the sample open at the start) we prepend the state's own wires to the text. Idea: `check({ wires, overlay })` or `world.ask(question)` that evaluates denies on the world plus the question without telling anything.
9. **Change cost.** About 0.2-0.7 ms per patch for a few hundred wires (the copy on the first write after a mark, then the rules' pass). Idea: a patch that only changes values no rule reads (the JSON of the focus) should skip the pass: per-slot `index=false` / `rules=false`.

- **Known limits.** Starting a tic-tac-toe game is not a timeline entry (the game wire is in the base, nothing changes), so it is not undone by itself; the age limit of six messages stays outside a run of undos; a new world must be named in `session.conversation`'s `compose=` to join the conversation.

## 2026-11-11 geo.map and data.charts on the matrix (worker agent)
- Both worlds say `reasoner=matrix`, `undo=`/`redo=` (the session timeline; `ownUndo` of the first version was dropped after the rebase), `kb=lookup`; `ops` unchanged in behaviour. test-world ops/matrix: geo.map 441/459, data.charts 267/277; all worlds pass in both modes; `npm test` green; Chrome 84/84. dump-turns before/after on 4,591 turns: the only differing turns are the known random ones (music, avatar), all 501 map/charts turns identical. bench group music/draw/map/charts p50 ops/matrix 10.22/10.56 before, 9.56/10.11 after (shared machine, noisy).
- Host (spec 15 section "geo.map and data.charts on the matrix"): undo/redo via `undo=`/`redo=`/`isView` and a fix so that an undo in the same message takes back that message's change first; `kb=lookup` + `ctx.host.kb(id)` (static index, no kb wires in the instance).
- Not done: "assumptions said once" for charts (default type chosen in ops); highlight-exists deny and derived within/borders relations (need kb wires in the instance, which cost 6x per turn).
### Library improvements
1. **Copy-on-write grows with world size.** 1,250 kb wires: turn 37 ms vs 6 ms (profile: `_detach`, `sets`, GC). Minimal example: load 1,300 wires, fork, tell one wire, time it. Idea: structural sharing (persistent map or frozen base layer with an overlay), so the cost is O(changed).
2. **External fact source.** Rules/denies cannot read a big table without holding it as wires. Idea: a host-provided provider for a type (`World({ facts: { 'geo.map.city': (where) => wires } })`) matched like wires; example: the `city_in` deny over the 1,000 cities.
3. **Redo of a rewind.** `rewind(mark)` has no redo; the host keeps the undo function (`redo=`). Idea: `rewind` returns a token, `replay(token)` re-applies the undone delta (or named timelines).
4. **Bulk sync of a collection.** Each world re-implements a 100-line diff (`adopt`). Idea: `world.sync(prefix|type, wires)` computing retract/set/tell by id in one transaction.
5. **JSON value kind.** Flat keys plus an `obj` string duplicate state. Idea: `kind=json` with path variables in patterns (`center.lat=?lat`).
6. **Tier from the host.** A value told by an operation cannot be marked `default`, so "I assumed a bar chart" is impossible. Idea: a tier annotation in told text.
7. **Aggregates with ties.** `argmax` ties report ambiguous in queries; in rules we emitted tie sets with `eq` over `max`. Idea: `ties=all` returning every pick.
8. **Host (ours):** a `conflicting` refusal needs a template per operation (48 + 45 generated); a generic `for=core.request status=conflicting` template would remove them.
## 2026-11-11 games.chess and media.music on the matrix (worker agent, wave 3 of "every world moves to sopworld")
- **Done, one commit each** (spec 15, "Stage 2, continued: games.chess and media.music on the matrix"; per-world rows in `sop_world_status.md`). `ops` behaves as before: `ops.mjs` of both worlds only gained `export` on the helpers the matrix shares (behaviour byte for byte; the literal file bytes differ by those keywords, as in the earlier worlds). Tests ops | matrix: games.chess 257 | 269, media.music 401 | 413; `tests/reasoner-chess-music.test.mjs` (17 tests); Chrome scenarios `chess` and `music` in `scripts/test-webchat-matrix.mjs`.
- **games.chess.** The game is wires (pieces, plies, `chess.legal`, `chess.drawn`, resignation, agreed draw); 52 rules and denies decide the turn, check, checkmate, stalemate, material, captured pieces and every refusal that names a piece and a square ("Your bishop on c1 is blocked by your pawn on d2.", "... is pinned: moving it would leave your king in check.", "It is not your turn."). The rules agree with chess.js on 137 000 candidate moves of random games (0 differences; `tests/reasoner-chess-music.test.mjs` runs 60 000). Move generation, notation, the engine and castling / en passant / promotion / repetition stay in functions: the rules can check a given move, not generate moves.
- **media.music.** The player, the melody queue and where each setting came from are wires; denies for a tempo outside 30..240, a key that has no signature ("There is no H major.", "There is no G# major.") and an instrument the player does not have; the tempo marking as rules; defaults assumed and said once; "why?" and undo in the world's words. The sound engine stays in functions.
- **Host additions (generic, small).** An action's result may carry `effects` (queued as the operation's effects: `Interpreter._libraryExtras`) and `decline: true` (the request goes back to the world's operations); actions get `ctx.live` (`request(action, args)`, `batch`) to ask another world, e.g. the active tab; an action that changed the wires of a world with `adopt` counts as that world's turn for the generic "undo that" words; the engine's observation is adopted (`Interpreter.observe` calls `adoptState`), so the engine's move reaches the rules.
- **Speed.** Chess turn p50 7.2 ms (ops) vs 9.1 ms (matrix), music 7.5 to 8.0 vs 9.1 to 9.3; `bench-reasoner.mjs --reps 3` before / after in `sop_world_status.md` (the other groups are unchanged). The matrix is not at ops speed on these two worlds: the rest of the cost is the library's per-turn bookkeeping and the patch that takes a changed state over; ideas below.

### Library improvements (from games.chess and media.music; for the library session)
1. **Expression calcs and geometry builtins.** The test "is this piece strictly between those two squares" is 17 `core.calc` and 9 `core.check` wires per rule (a cross product, two min / max boxes, an `abs` distance). Minimal example: `@b core.calc out=?cross expr="(?pf-?ef)*(?kr-?er)-(?pr-?er)*(?kf-?ef)"` and `@c core.check expr="?pf>=?minf and ?pf<=?maxf"`, or builtins `between(x, a, b)` (inclusive) and `sign(x)`. It would cut `worlds/games.chess/matrix.sop` by about a third.
2. **`replace` in a patch.** `applyPatch({ retract: ['chess.g'], wires: '@chess.g chess.game ...' })` is refused as `duplicate-id` (the wires are ingested before the retract), so a world that keeps a record in a wire invents revisions (`chess.g_1`, `music.p_2`). A `replace: [{ id, attrs }]` entry (or retract-then-tell order) keeps ids stable, which `explain` / undo marks also like.
3. **Ranking of denies.** `world.check` lists violations unordered, and a refused move usually breaks several rules at once (the piece cannot move like that AND something is in the way AND the king is left in check). The host ranks by regexes over the rule id. Minimal example: `core.deny rank=2` (lower first) or `world.check(...).first`.
4. **Lazy rules.** Every position change re-derives the whole attack structure (rays, shields, hits: the check status) although nobody asks for it until a move is tried: it is most of the 2 ms a chess turn costs over ops. `core.rule lazy=true` (derive on a query or a `check`, not in the chase) or a rule group switched on by the presence of a wire (`when=$chess.try` already makes the try-rules free; the always-on part is the one that costs).
5. **Per-attribute tier.** The library knows that a value came from a default, but a retract and a tell turn it into a told value, so music keeps three `*src` attributes by hand and a `music.said` wire for "said once". Minimal example: `@p music.player tempo=100~default` or `applyPatch({ wires, tiers: { 'music.p.tempo': 'default' } })`, and `world.tier(id, key)` readable in a `core.check` (`fn=tier args=$p args=tempo`).
6. **Undo by unit.** `undo=` rewinds a whole turn; the world's own undo took back one note. `rewind(mark, { only: 'music.note' })` or `core.undo unit=music.note` (the last wire of that type told in the turn).
7. **A table from a lexicon.** The 24 instrument names and the 30 key signatures are generated into `matrix.sop` and also stand in `lexicon.sop` / `theory.mjs`. `core.table from=lex.media.music.instruments type=music.alias key=name` would load the words as facts, and the matrix would not need a generator.
8. **A json / nested slot.** The state a tab keeps beside the game (the question that waits, highlights, the score, the undo history) is a JSON string in `rest` / `data` attributes; it is opaque to rules and costs a parse per turn. `kind=json` with `world.get(id).json` (read-only structured clone), or nested wires with `of=` parents that `ofType` can read as one object.
9. **Enumerating candidates.** Legal move generation would be the rules over the 4096 pairs of squares (62 000 `chess.try` checks of 0.5 ms each, about 30 s: far too slow to generate on every turn, fine to verify). A generator (`core.each ?a in $square ?b in $square then $try`) or backward chaining at query time would let the engine ask the library "which moves are legal here" and a rule-built move list would carry its own reasons ("pinned", "blocked").
10. **`act` bookkeeping.** About 0.5 ms a turn goes to `rests` / `assumptions` / `_complete` for actions that read no cell: `core.action pure=true`. The effect events of a `music.play` are cloned twice (the action's result, then the host's queue): an `effects` field the library passes through untouched.
11. **Not tried (honest).** The position as a *fluent* (the library's sequence with states changed by events: pieces initiated and terminated by move events), which would make `chess.piece` derived instead of told. A hundred plies of fluent states per query looked too slow for the check status of every turn; a measurement is the next step if idea 4 lands.

## 2026-11-11 art.draw and diagram.sketch on the matrix (worker agent)
- Both worlds say `reasoner=matrix`, `undo=`/`redo=` (the session timeline), `source=`; `ops` unchanged in behaviour (art.draw's `ops.mjs` got one `export { ... }` line; diagram.sketch's is untouched). The drawing, layout and geometry engines stay in code; the scene state and its validation moved: wires per object / shape / arrow / group / selection, denies with reasons, derived counts (art.draw: things and kinds; diagram.sketch: degree), a target guard (art.draw: "I can not find a green circle in your picture."; diagram.sketch: "That arrow already connects A and B."), undo and redo in the world's words with `isView` (a selection is not a step) and `stateKey`.
- test-world ops / matrix: art.draw 562 / 562, diagram.sketch 756 / 756 (all worlds pass in both modes). `npm test` green after three adjustments of existing tests: `tests/reasoner.test.mjs` (the list of matrix worlds and the parity counts), `tests/draw.test.mjs` (no `history` key in matrix mode; the timeline is bounded at 40) and `tests/discourse-memory.test.mjs` (**undo after a restart**: the library's timeline is session memory, so a restored art.draw session has the picture but nothing to undo; the test now asserts the ops behaviour on an ops engine and the matrix limit). New tests: `tests/reasoner-draw-diagram.test.mjs` (17), Chrome scenarios `draw` and `diagram` in `scripts/test-webchat-draw-matrix.mjs` hooked into `scripts/test-webchat-matrix.mjs`.
- Measured. Turn time, 16-turn probe sessions (5 sessions, p50 ms ops / matrix, loaded machine): art.draw 7.8 / 9.8, diagram.sketch 8.9 / 12.5; bench group numbers in `sop_world_status.md`. Cost per turn is the state takeover (`adoptState` 2.1 ms draw / 1.4 ms diagram, `_state` 0.6 ms, the guard of a connect 0.3 ms). Wires: a board of 32 things is 62 wires and 4.7 kB, a diagram of 20 shapes and 14 arrows 83 wires and 5.9 kB. Scaling on synthetic boards (told all / one thing moved / every thing moved): 50 things 3 / 1 / 3 ms, 200 things 9 / 2.5 / 15 ms, 1,000 things 39 / 11 / 91 ms; diagram with 400 shapes and 399 arrows 47 / 8 / 59 ms. Large point lists are not wires: shape geometry is computed from `kind` and `size` by scene.mjs and the engine's routes are never state.
- Differences from ops (intended): the counters (next id, opener cycle, revision) are rewound by an undo; the refusal of a repeated arrow is the library's sentence; "undo" on a chess, music or editor tab is the timeline's newest turn instead of the board's `SHARED` hand-off.
- Honest verdict. art.draw: the library adds the invariants, the counts, the refusal with a reason and an exact redo; the engine (1,574 + 477 lines) is untouched, about 30 lines of questions are answered from derived wires. diagram.sketch: the library adds little: the engine already refuses what cannot be and has its own Datalog reasoner for every question; the denies are a guarantee, the repeated-arrow guard changes one sentence, the degree rules are read by no operation (they would be the right base for degree, neighbours, reachable and cycle if the engine's reader handed the library the names it resolves). Both cost 2-3.6 ms per turn over ops for it.
### Library improvements (art.draw, diagram.sketch)
1. **Group-by aggregates are quadratic when anchored on a member.** `core.calc fn=count over=$o` with the rule's `when=$o1` on a member of the group evaluates the aggregate once per member: 1,000 stars, one moved, 433 ms; anchored on 26 static `kindfact` facts, 11 ms. Minimal: `@o art.draw.object kind=?k` / `@o1 art.draw.object kind=?k` / `@c core.calc out=?n fn=count over=$o` / `@r core.rule when=$o1 with=$c then=...`. Idea: aggregate once per group key (dedupe by the variables shared with the anchor), or `core.group of=$o by=?k` yielding one wire per distinct key.
2. **A source of distinct values.** The static kind facts (26 lines) only exist so that an empty kind has its 0 and the anchor is not a member. Idea: `core.distinct of=$o key=?k` as an anchor, plus `default=0` for empty groups (same as improvement 3 of the docs worlds).
3. **Referential integrity as a slot kind.** Six of the nine denies of diagram.sketch and two of art.draw are "this value is the key of a wire that is not there" (an arrow end, a group member, a selection, an "it"), each a pair of patterns, a `not=` and a hand-written reason. Idea: `@s core.slot of=diagram.sketch.edge key=to kind=ref to=diagram.sketch.shape.name`, refused with a generated reason ("?to is not a shape") unless the world words it.
4. **Unique keys.** "Two things with one id" is a pair pattern with `neq`; it is cheap (433 vs 406 ms when removed) but is boilerplate: `core.slot unique=true`.
5. **Labels on marks.** "OK, I undid it (colored it blue)" needs the label of the step that was undone; the library's `mark()` carries none, so the world copies the operations' label into a wire (`art.draw.scene.label`) and `undoWords` reads it from the state before. Idea: `mark(label)` and `world.marks()` listing `{ id, label }`, so `undoWords` can ask the library.
6. **A timeline that survives a restart.** After a watchdog restart the page restores the world's state but the library has no marks, so "undo" says there is nothing to undo (ops kept the board's 50 snapshots in the state). Idea: `world.toJSON({ timeline: true })` / `World.fromJSON`, or `world.adopt(state)` as the first mark.
7. **A reason that lists.** The engine's refusal "There is no shape named "Z". Shapes on the canvas: A, B." and the board's hint "But there is a red circle and 2 blue squares, so you can say "the circle"" need a string aggregation over several wires. Idea: `core.calc fn=join over=$p args=?name sep=", "` (or `?names:list` in a deny's reason).
8. **Sets of values nobody reads.** Moving every thing of a 1,000-thing board is 91 ms: two `sets` per wire (`x` and the duplicate in `obj`) and a pass of the rules for values no rule reads. Idea (as improvement 9 of the docs worlds, now with a number): `core.slot index=false` for `obj`, `x`, `y`, and a patch whose keys are all unindexed skips the pass.
9. **Host (ours).** 89 more generated `conflicting` templates (art.draw 33, diagram.sketch 56): the generic template for `status=conflicting` asked for before is now worth 89 lines; the `adopt` function is the same 50 lines for the fourth and fifth time (improvement 4 of geo.map and data.charts).

## 2026-11-11 - ui.shell, ui.avatar and ui.help on the matrix (wave 3 of "every world moves to sopworld")
- Three commits (spec 15, "ui.shell, ui.avatar and ui.help on the matrix"): ui.shell (05dafc8), ui.avatar (e6877d0), ui.help (da80e7f). The operations of the three worlds keep running on the session state (`adopt` / `toState`, as the document worlds and user.profile); ops mode is unchanged (scripted-session parity tests, `test-world all` both modes, the Chrome language and matrix scenarios). The knowledge bases are generated (`scripts/gen-ui-kb.mjs`, checked by `tests/reasoner-ui.test.mjs`): the tabs, the words that name them, the games, the languages and the Settings tabs from the manifests (shell); the characters, their parts and colors, the color names, which spoken part names which color, the start look of an AV character from the DiceBear lexicons and the tables of `ops.mjs` (avatar); each area's capability sentence and the words that name the area from the manifests and the canonical examples (help).
- **Generic host additions** (all optional, spec 15 "Host additions of the third wave"): `passive=true` (a world whose state follows the others: the shell's tab; what another world's request changes in it or its creation by one is not a step of that turn, so the editor's "undo" does not switch the tab back), `turns[i].moved` and a world's own "undo" that only walks back its own turns (before it rewound whatever turn came last and said "I undid the last change" on an unchanged editor), "undo" typed while the open tab's world has nothing to take back falls through to the last turn of another world or a shell setting (not to the switch to that tab), `guard` may name a `reason` (`unknown_argument` keeps "There is no weather tab." a weak reading for the dispatcher), a request is the library's only when the matrix declares the action (no instance and no `act` call for the operations' actions of an adopt world), `guard` makes the instance lazily, `adoptState(session, ownId)`, a string value read back unescaped (a kb sentence with quotes came out as `\"...\"`).
- **Tests.** ui.shell 64 / 64 (ops / matrix, unchanged), ui.avatar 327 / 327, ui.help 87 / 87 (83 + 4 new for the new request, both modes); `tests/reasoner-ui.test.mjs` 17 matrix tests (7 shell, 6 avatar, 3 help, 1 all); Chrome `shell`, `avatar`, `help` in `scripts/test-webchat-ui-matrix.mjs` (hooked into `scripts/test-webchat-matrix.mjs`: 101/101), `test-webchat-language.mjs` passes. Numbers and the per-world rows: sop_world_status.md.
- **Things that bit.** (1) Every wire of a kb costs about 10 microseconds per turn (the library copies the world at the first write after each turn's mark and validates what a change touches): the first avatar with the 581 variants and 251 palette entries as wires cost 170 ms per nine turns against 76 for ops; with 243 kb wires it costs 100. The variants and palette entries are not wires, so the rules cannot say "this variant belongs to this character" (the lexicons enforce it). (2) A world whose state is rebuilt from the wires after every change loses what the operations filled in lazily (the six AV characters from the lexicons, 2 ms each time): the kb holds their start look and `toState` puts them back; the state key leaves the characters that are not shown out, so a state nobody changed has the key of the empty instance and no instance is made. (3) `owns()` made an instance and called `act` for every request of every matrix world, only to hear "unsupported": a question about the avatar's name created the avatar's instance. (4) The default tab is the avatar and the avatar has `undo=`: every bare "undo" went to the avatar's own undo ("nothing to undo") even when the last turn was a purchase; the fall-through above fixed it, and the RO regression `undo-own-words` caught it. (5) `kb.sop` of a platform world must not extend `core.state` (a test counts the descendants); the validator wants the kb types in the legend, and the avatar's legend is generated: `legend.extra.sop` is appended by `scripts/legend-from-dicebear.mjs`. (6) `setWorldState` (a click) is taken over at the end of the next turn, not at once: a host patch is not a step because the world is passive.
- **Not done.** Redo for the avatar and the shell: the only `redo` pattern is the editor's, a new one would be a new shared phrase and an operation in `ops` mode as well (see the improvement notes: a generic "redo that" in the dialog layer, like "undo that"). Assumptions ("I assumed ...") for the avatar's defaults: the assumption mechanism says a default tier value an answer rests on, and no answer of these worlds is a library query, so there is nothing to word (kids.time precedent). Variant and palette membership as rules (cost, above). The shell's `handsFree` implies `listening` is not a rule: the host sets one without the other (`app.mjs`). A tab id is not checked on the state: the host names tabs as it likes (`avatar` and `robot`, test tabs). Romanian: a few new shell sentences are in `replies.en-ro.mjs`; the avatar's refusals ("A robot has no mouth color. Mouths can be ...") and the area sentences of the help stay English in a Romanian session (the reply is kept whole in English, never mixed).

### Library improvements (for the library session, from ui.shell, ui.avatar and ui.help; not repeated: opaque values, bound variables in `why`, anchors for aggregates, rank, transient wires, upsert, string checks, dry run with overlay, change cost, the `_detach` copy)
1. **A shared, frozen knowledge base.** Measured here: the cost of a turn grows with the number of kb wires, about 10 microseconds per wire per turn (the avatar's kb: 1,140 wires 170 ms per nine turns, 243 wires 100, 73 wires about 85, ops 76). The kb never changes after the load, yet every mark/fork copies it and every validation may scan it. Idea: wires loaded from a text declared `core.kb` (or `world.freeze()` after the base is built) are **shared by every fork and never copied or revalidated**, only indexed for lookup; `type=` slots can still point at them. With that the 581 variants and 251 palette entries of the avatar would be wires and `part value=?v` would be refused by a rule when `?v` is not a variant of the character, with the reason.
2. **Membership in a list kept in one wire.** "Is this word one of the 67 color names?" costs 67 wires (or a refusal from the schema's `enum`, which has no `why`). Idea: `core.check fn=member args=?x args=$names.key` where `names` is a single wire with many values of one key (`@cn colornames name=red name=blue ...`), or `core.slot ... in=$names.key why="..."`. One wire, O(1) per check, our wording.
3. **A wording for the violation of a `type=` slot.** A `type=` reference to a wire that is not there is refused with a generic `dangling-ref`; we want "There is no X for the mouth of the robot." Idea: `why=` on the slot, used for `domain`, `type` and `dangling-ref`, with `?value` and `?key` bound.
4. **List rendering in `why`.** "Mouths can be grill, speaker, line, smile and zigzag." needs the list as one string in the kb (generated), because a deny cannot join several values. Idea: `?list` bound to all values of a many-valued key, joined by the world's `join` filter (`?mouths:and`), or `core.calc fn=join` (same as the animals note: no aggregate joins values).
5. **An instance's birth and the load of shared wires are not a change.** `world.stamp` moves when a new instance replays the shared wires, so a turn that only created an instance looks like a step (host workaround: `moved` per turn, and `passive`). Idea: the stamp moves only for changes made after the base and the replay; or `world.mark()` taken after them.
6. **Untracked writes.** `passive` needs a patch that is applied but is not part of the undo history ("the host took the click over", "the dialog layer noted the last unparsed sentence"). Idea: `applyPatch(patch, { track: false })` (the wires are written, `mark`/`rewind` skip them, and a rewind to an older mark leaves them as they are), which is the per-call form of the "transient wires" idea.
7. **A default that is a whole object.** `default=` fills one value of one key; the start look of an AV character is a group of 15 wires with defaults chosen by rules over the variants (first variant, none when optional). We generate six JSON blobs in the kb and unpack them in `toState`. Idea: `core.default of=type then=$template` (a template wire group created when a wire of the type is told and the group is missing), the structural form of the default tier.
8. **Which cells of these wires are defaults?** `_rests` is private; the host reads it to say "I assumed ...". For worlds whose answers are computed in code (adopt worlds) there is no query, so no assumption is ever said. Idea: a public `world.assumptions(ids)` that returns the default/guessed cells of the given wires, so a world's `fns.mjs` can say its own defaults once.
9. **A generic "redo that".** `undo that` is generic (the dialog layer rewinds the library); redo only exists as a world's own action (`redo=`) with a pattern of the world. The marks the undo kept are the library's: a generic "redo" (and the `world.redo()` of improvement 5 of the document worlds) would give every matrix world redo with no pattern, no operation and no phrase of its own.


## RESTORE-1: the matrix undo timeline survives a watchdog restart

`DialogSession.memoryJSON()` now carries `rs` (`ReasonerSession.timelineJSON`: per instance the library log of the instance and of every mark of the kept 40 turns, entries stored once, plus the timeline, shared log, told, said); `restoreMemory` replays it into fresh instances (`restoreJSON`, marks re-taken from the replayed worlds). Spec 15 "The timeline survives a restart". Measured on a 40-turn session (conversation + art.draw): 48 KB record, 0.8 ms to write per turn, 58 ms to restore; capped at 1.5 MB (oldest turns dropped first, `dropped` reported). Not restored: the redo stack (functions). Tests: `tests/reasoner-restore.test.mjs` (undo to the end identical with and without restart, both reasoners), `tests/discourse-memory.test.mjs`, Chrome scenario `restart-undo` in `scripts/test-webchat-matrix.mjs`. Library improvement (proposal 3 of questions.md Q1): the host uses `world.log` and the underscore `World._replay` and rebuilds marks by hand; a library-level `snapshotJSON({ marks })` / `restore(json, { base })` would replace it. Note: a game action can rewrite an entry of the base log (tictactoe), so the record keeps `p` (shared prefix) per log.

## E1: error audit of the current formalization (FORMALIZE-FROM-WORLDS, measurement only)

`scripts/e1-collect.mjs` runs operational, operational-ro, dialogues, gold (pilot-300), loose-words, typos, real phrases (09c/d/e with expectations, unjudged log rows) and the 09f mobile export through the shared pipeline in `rules` and in the shipped `encoder+n1` (onnxruntime-node 1.20.1 installed in the scratchpad; both modes ran). `scripts/e1-audit.mjs` classifies every non-right case (a..h) and writes `training/results/e1-audit-2026-11-12.{json,md}` (table per set, per world, 5 examples per class, the reviewer's named cases re-run). Human decisions are in `scripts/e1-overrides.json` (18 operational cases); a, f (RO-vs-EN pairing), h (alternative splits read by the rule formalizer), c (open tab belongs to the expected world) are mechanical; b is by elimination. Not done: an overlay check of candidate readings against world instances (that is E2); the c share is a floor.

Key numbers (rules mode): English core (operational + dialogues) 131 non-right, 75.6% c+d+e+h (h 42, e 39, c 18, d 0), 21.4% a+b+f, 4 expectation wrong. English incl. gold paraphrases and real phrases: 242 errors, 58.3% c+d+e+h, 35.5% a+b+f (b 51 is the largest class there). Romanian operational: 451 non-right, 349 have a right English source (f), so 80% a+b+f and only 18.2% c+d+e+h; 125 of the 349 have words left untranslated, 224 are translated into in-vocabulary English that the rules do not read. In encoder+n1 the English core falls to 99 errors (66.7% c+d+e+h; h drops from 42 to 8), operational-ro to 368. Encoder+n1 vs rules: 242 fixed, 81 broken; 130 cases are wrong only in encoder+n1 (loose-words 62, typos 30, operational-ro 21, dialogues 12). Conclusion: world-aware reading (E2) pays in English compounds and follow-ups; the Romanian gap needs the translator route or generated patterns (E3).

## RO-TRANSLATE-FALLBACK: rules first, the on-device Translator as a bounded safety net (both directions)

- **Order and modes.** `createSwitchTranslator.fallbackMode()`: strategy `auto` = rules and templates first, Translator as fallback; `browser-api` = the old order (kept as an explicit strategy; the bridge rows of `scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs` now use it); `rules` = never the Translator. New: `viaBrowser(text, {from, to})` (Translator only, bridge deadline, `checkTranslation`, and for en to ro no English words left), `canTranslate`, `note`.
- **Input** (`DialogSession._send` wraps `_sendCore`). A poor Romanian reading (unparsed clause 3, unused part 1 each, best effort 2, message left in Romanian by the rules 2) asks the Translator; the session is restored to the state before the first reading, the clean English is read with the English rules, and the second reading wins only with fewer trouble points (else the first is restored unchanged). `trace.via = 'translator-in'`.
- **Output** (`translateParts`). A run the rules cannot say goes to the Translator (`translator-out`), our own English reading in "I took that as" and the examples too; what nothing can say becomes "Nu am încă o formulare în română pentru acest răspuns." plus the user's quoted words (`ro-generic`); a fully rules-translated reply is `rules-translate`. Max 6 Translator calls per turn. The plain `createTranslator()` (CLI, tests) keeps the RD5 behaviour. Host strings: `DialogSession.observeIn` (used by the engine worker) translates the engine's own chess move etc.
- **Logging.** `usage[].via`, `reply.trace.via` / `replyVia`, phrase-log `via`. Spec: specs/06 "Translator as a fallback".
- **Checker fix.** A Romanian statement starting with "e", "este", "are", "ai" ("e ora 25") was taken for a question; only true question words (ce, cine, unde, cum, câți ...) or a final "?" count now.
- **Fake.** `scripts/lib/fake-browser-apis.mjs` offers ro to en and en to ro (`pairs`), from a small table (`FAKE_RO_EN`, `FAKE_EN_RO`) then the rules; `createFakeTranslation` for node.
- **Small fixes.** "ce zi este azi" (both directions); Romanian quotes „…” (parts and templates; the tests and `ro-matrix-sessions.json` expectations follow); the refusal quotes the user's word („dragoni”, `userWordMap`); `ce poți face pe hartă?`, "înapoi", the chess engine lines and "Ești cu albele și muți primul", song titles kept („Twinkle, Twinkle, Little Star”); chess undo keeps "We are back at the start." (`worlds/games.chess/fns.mjs`, `undoWords`).
- **Measure** (`node scripts/measure-ro-fallback.mjs --reasoner matrix|ops`, Romanian operational set, 2,480 messages, oracle Translator = the English source message; an upper bound). matrix: right 81.8% -> 88.6%, wrong 78 -> 53, not understood 325 -> 191, replies shown in English 311 -> 0 (203 through the Translator, 139 as the fixed Romanian line, the rest by rules). ops: right 82.1% -> 89.0%, wrong 61 -> 40, not understood 334 -> 196, English 305 -> 0. Time (the fake answers at once): p50 20 -> 24 ms matrix, 20 -> 20 ms ops; p95 97 -> 151 ms and 130 -> 156 ms; add the real Translator's latency (up to 400 ms per call in the page, 700 ms cap in the worker).
- **Open.** 139 / 133 replies still end as the fixed Romanian line without a real en to ro Translator: the families are in `check-ro-replies` (add templates). "mixed" 2 cases are older.

## RE-VENDOR sopworld 0.7.1 then 0.9.0, workarounds removed (2026-11-12)

- Commits: re-vendor 0.7.1 (93362d7); canComplete, replace in place, reason filters, aggregate; kb as wires; adopt replace in six more worlds; kind=blob; re-vendor 0.9.0 (f062052, build 807d8bde76f3) with the webchat rebuilt. Rebased on main 48ecaab (RO-TRANSLATE-FALLBACK, E3); chess "undo" still says "We are back at the start."
- Numbers and the table per world group: `sop_world_status.md`, "Re-vendor sopworld 0.7.1 and 0.9.0". The matrix gap goes from +3.0 to about +2.0 ms p50 (x1.28 to x1.22, paired runs); half in the four matrix worlds, none in games.
- Gates: `npm test` 1415 pass (the one failure after the 0.9.0 copy was the stale webchat build), `test-world all` both reasoners clean, `test-webchat-matrix` 180/180, `npm run test:browser` exit 0, `dump-turns` identical except random turns.
- Changed behaviour that is a gain: kids.animals value-mode query of a many-valued key is `supported` (it was `conflicting`).

### Library improvements after 0.7.1 (minimal repros)
1. **Delta snapshots.** `world.toJSON({ timeline: true })` writes the whole log of the world and of each mark, base included. Repro: `rs = session.isession.rs` after eight turns of the PSys2 session in `tests/reasoner-restore.test.mjs`; for each instance `JSON.stringify(world.toJSON({ timeline: true }).log).length` is 507 kB for geo.map, 235 kB for session.conversation, 65 kB for ui.help; `World.fromJSON({ format: 'sopworld', version: 1, log }, options)` takes 96 ms for the geo.map log, and every open mark is a further full replay. Wanted: `toJSON({ timeline: true, since: baseWorld })` (entries after the base's own log) and `World.fromJSON(data, { base })` (forks the base, replays the rest, restores marks as forks of the same). The host would then drop `world.log` and `_replay` from `restoreJSON`.
2. **Timeline across worlds.** `rewind(mark)` returns no handle and `redo()` only follows `undo()`. Repro: `m = w.mark(); w.tell('@a x'); w.rewind(m); w.redo()` gives `nothing-to-redo`. Wanted: `rewind(mark)` keeps what it took back for `redo()` (R7.1 `rewind({ only })` may cover it), so `redos` and `isView` in the host can go.
3. **`core.aggregate by=` with a declared group set.** Repro: `@k core.aggregate over=$o by=?k count=?c then=$t`, with objects of kind circle only: no tally for `square`, where the host needs 0. Wanted `groups=$kindfact` or `by=?k within=$kinds` giving 0 for a group with no member.
4. **Fixed per-turn cost of an instance** (not a library defect proven): games show +2.9 ms p50 over ops in every run; `bench-reasoner.mjs --instrument` should name the part; ask the library only if it is `fork` / `mark` / `complete`.

## E3b: descriptions as wires and keys, `core.word`, Romanian pack (2026-11-12)

- Commits: 855088f (corrections 1 and 2); the Romanian pack, scripts, results and webchat rebuild in the commit after it. Results: `training/results/e3b-2026-11-12.{json,md}`; spec 03 section 2c rewritten.
- **1. One language.** `core.describe`, `core.describe.arg` (one per argument: `of`, `name`, `class`, `optional=true`, `qty`), `core.describe.words` (one per language; repeated keys, `prep.<arg>`, `sample.<slot>`). No notation inside a value. Generated patterns of kids.shop, geo.map and docs.editor are **byte-identical** before and after (`cmp` of `gen-from-descriptions.mjs` output: 1367 / 911 / 558 lines).
- **2. `core.word`.** Every word is a `core.word lang=en text=.. means=<op>|<op>.<arg>` wire (173 / 344 / 247 wires), referred to by role keys; the loader checks the `means`. What `core.word` cannot carry: the role of the word, the order of alternatives, descriptions that share an op but not its words, argument roles that are not matrix keys (the library would warn `unknown-word`), and a canonical value per surface form for slot values in another language.
- **3. Romanian (measured proposal; defaults and AGENTS.md unchanged).** `lang/ro/frames.sop`, `lang/ro/<world>.sop`, `lang/ro/<world>.entities.sop` (kids.shop 11 of 11 descriptions, geo.map 9 of 27, docs.editor 6 of 25; 228 patterns; 301 entity words), opt-in `PSYS_GEN_LANG=ro`. On the 41 E1 f-cases of the three worlds: translator route 0 right, 16 wrong, 25 not understood; fake-Translator oracle 13 / 10 / 18; pack-first with translator fallback and a joiner guard (lenient: the user's own text stays Romanian) 17 / 12 / 12. On all 360 Romanian cases of the three worlds: 309 / 26 / 25 (T), 322 / 20 / 18 (oracle), 326 / 22 / 12 (pack, lenient, guard). New wrong answers vs T: strict 20 (all "Cumparaturi" against the English "Shopping"), lenient 0; without the joiner guard 8 (the English clause and list splitters do not know "si"). English cases with the pack loaded: 0 of 236 changed. E3 on gold-para class b by name: 34 cases, only 4 in described worlds, no change under `add` or `only`.
- Findings for the engine: a per-language joiner list for the clause and list splitters; a per-language surface form in lexicons (the pre-pass cannot rewrite a word that is also a role word: titlu, listă, paragraf); Romanian messages must be read by Romanian patterns only (English patterns misread them).
- Gates: `npm test` green (1418 pass, 0 fail), `test-world all` both reasoners clean, `build-webchat.mjs` run.
- **Question for the user:** may a Romanian language pack (`lang/ro/`, outside the worlds) hold Romanian words? Worlds stay English-only either way; the pack is opt-in and AGENTS.md is unchanged until you decide.
## E2: world-aware reading, veto first then choice (2026-11-12)

- **What was built** (option `worldAware: off | veto | choose | observe | veto-strict | choose-strict`, default off; `PSYS2_WORLD_AWARE`, `scripts/e1-collect.mjs --world-aware`). Spec: `specs/16-world-aware-reading.md`. Code: `src/dialog/world-aware.mjs`; `ReasonerSession.score` and `peek` (read-only, the library's `score` and `rank`); a `pick` hook in the rule formalizer (`readGroup`); optional read-only `checkReferents` hooks in `art.draw` and `diagram.sketch`. Tests: `tests/world-aware.test.mjs` (12). Measurement: `scripts/e2-world-aware.mjs`; results `training/results/e2-world-aware-2026-11-12.{json,md}` (every changed case, per set, both modes).
- **Veto reasons** (default `veto`): `no-referent` (the world has no such thing: "delete the dragon" lists what IS in the picture; "connect Start to End" with no End: "There is no shape called End. Shall I add it?" and a "yes" does it), `no-game` (a move in a game that is not running, asked from another tab), `words-best-effort` (a guess of loose words that leaves content words out becomes "Did you mean ...?"). "switch to klingon" answers with the languages from the ui.shell kb. `words-partial` (the partial-understanding window) is only in the `-strict` modes: it loses more right answers than it prevents wrong ones.
- **Numbers** (about 8,430 judged messages; right / wrong / not understood). rules: off 7263 / 431 / 650, veto 7261 / 398 / 685, choose 7267 / 399 / 683, veto-strict 7194 / 361 / 790. encoder+n1 (shipped): off 7436 / 482 / 485, veto 7439 / 456 / 509, choose 7439 / 456 / 510, veto-strict 7386 / 420 / 598. veto against off: wrong to not understood 28 (rules) and 22 (encoder+n1), wrong to right 5 and 6, right to not understood 7 and 3. **Gate met: no new wrong in veto and choose, both modes** (the first runs had 6 and 1: the sibling hold, and a `no-game` veto of the spurious reading of a correction, both fixed). veto-strict has one new wrong (a dialogue turn that rests on a dropped earlier turn).
- **Named cases.** Fixed in veto/choose: "fă părul roșu" (no longer "make it red": now not understood, the reading that dropped "părul" lost), "connect Start to End", "delete the dragon", "switch to klingon", "put it in the top right corner" on the Drawing tab (veto: no game is running; choose: moves the house). Not changed, and why: "give it a hat made of cheese", "center and what is 3 times 3", "colour Spain in" (now "There is no Spain in the picture", safe but the map has Spain), "add milk ... and also can penguins fly", "ce poți face pe hartă?", "înapoi": the right reading is not among the readings the formalizers found, or the world has no hook yet.
- **Library order against the host.** The library's default `rank` counts the answer a question cannot give yet as a lack: it would put a statement that tells nonsense ("a gretel goes what") before the question "what did gretel say". The host zeroes `lacks` for question readings (6 Romanian total questions differ; the router's reading was right in all six); `libraryDefault` is recorded in every pick. A move in an idle game comes last (14 reorderings applied; rules only). In encoder+n1 the library's order and the host's agree everywhere.
- **Honest limits.** (1) `score` returns `judged = false` for most readings: the matrices declare few actions (spec 15), so the library alone seldom separates two readings; `no-referent` and `no-game` read the worlds' hooks and the session context. (2) Choice is over the rules' top readings and the best-effort candidates only. Not done: the dispatcher's top-k (one reading per clause), the library's split candidates (class h: `interpret` needs `core.word` facts, none exist yet: E3 item 2), the dialogue layer's follow-up readings (the discourse layer mutates its state before it reads), the translator route. (3) Romanian is not E2's: the words the translator leaves of three letters or fewer count as function words, the rest as content. (4) The one word-level veto that works is the best-effort one; for the rest no cheap feature separates harmful from harmless dropped words (questions read as commands: 11 right against 6 wrong).
- **For the user (no default changed).** `veto` looks safe to turn on: it never added a wrong answer and turned 28 wrong answers into not understood (22) or right (6) in the shipped mode, for 3 right answers lost. `choose` adds a little on top. The world hooks are the part that scales: each world that writes `checkReferents` gets state-aware refusals; today two do.
## NAMESPACES: fact namespaces on sopworld 0.9.0 (2026-11-12)

- **Done.** (1) Every library instance is a World with its own namespace (`session.conversation`, each game, each tab); `src/reasoner/ns.mjs` hands it out with bare ids in and out (qualify what goes in, strip what comes out, `{ ns }` on queries) and gives the action functions of `fns.mjs` the same through `nsContext`; the timeline record is `v: 2` (the library log holds qualified ids; a v1 record is refused as unreadable). (2) Ids made in a turn carry it: `FormSession.turn`, `t12_w3` (the counter is still the session's). Tests: `tests/reasoner-namespaces.test.mjs` (two "I buy a ball" turns are two purchases with the turn in their ids; two draw objects of one name are two; the diagram refuses the second shape of a name; the library keeps two namespaces apart and merges inside one). (3) `shared=true` is declared for `core.agent` (what every instance reads); `reads=` is used by no world, so there is no `exports=` to write (the library's `exports=` is for `core.module`, which the worlds are not). (4) `world.warnings` of every instance, reported once per session: `reply.warnings`, debug view "Library warnings", phrase log `detail.warnings`. (5) Spec 15, "Namespaces".
- **Gates.** `npm test` 1,437 pass, 0 fail (loop guard included); `test-world all` clean in both reasoners; `build-webchat.mjs` run; Chrome `test-webchat-matrix` and `test:browser` below.
- **dump-turns before/after, 4,591 turns.** 4,262 turns identical; 277 differ only by the turn prefix in ids inside the state (`t1_w1` for `w1`); the 52 others are the known random turns (media.music picks a song, ui.avatar a seed: 5 + 1 + 46 turns) and 46 `ui.avatar` states whose pending request has a `span.end` 3 longer (the id grew by `t1_`). Intended differences: the ids, nothing else. Replies, statuses and every other state: identical.
- **Bench** (`bench-reasoner.mjs --reps 3`, 12,072 turns; the old tree from `git archive HEAD` and the new tree at the same time on the same loaded machine; p50 ms, ops / matrix, matrix minus ops): all 9.1 / 11.0, +1.9 before and 9.2 / 11.8, +2.7 after (second run on 700 cases per set: +2.1 -> +2.5). Groups, full run: four +2.1 -> +3.5, kids-other +1.6 -> +2.7, music/draw/map/charts +2.0 -> +2.7, games +2.7 -> +3.8, documents +1.8 -> +2.6, avatar/shell/editor +1.6 -> +1.7. The cost is the library's (`_nsUsed` adds the namespace to every match) plus the facade's walk over results; the facade is capped in depth and allocates only where an id is found.
- **Library items (sopworld 0.9.0), each with a minimal repro** (scripts in the session scratchpad; the numbers are what 0.9.0 does):
  1. `replace(id, text)` in a world with `ns` puts the new wire in the default namespace when the id in `text` is a bare one: `w = new World({ ns: 'A' }); w.load('@t core.type\n@t.k core.slot of=t key=k kind=number'); w.tell('@x t k=1'); w.replace('A::x', '@x t k=2'); [...w.wires.keys()]` gives `['x']`, expected `['A::x']`. (The host writes the qualified id.)
  2. The result of a `core.aggregate` is told in the default namespace, not in that of the counted wires, so a rule or deny that joins it with the namespace's facts never matches: `w = new World({ ns: 'A' })`, load `@block core.type`, a `core.aggregate over=$bk by=?k count=?c then=$hk` into `have kind=?k count=?c`, a deny `when=$w2 not=$wh2` (want / have by kind), tell `@b1 block kind=paragraph`: the `have` wire is `_hk_...` (default namespace) and `check` of a `want kind=paragraph` is refused ("There is no paragraph"). It passes when `have` is `shared=true`. (docs.editor therefore stays in the default namespace: `NO_NAMESPACE` in `ns.mjs`.)
  3. A type declared through `core.schema.map` (every type of our legends) ignores `shared=`, `transient=` and `unique=`: only a native `core.type` reads them (`!f &&` in the compile of types). `m.load('@map core.schema.map from=mytype as=type name=id shared=shared'); m.load('@agent mytype shared=true'); m._compiled().shared` is empty; with `@agent core.type shared=true` it holds `agent`. The map needs `shared=`, `transient=`, `unique=` fields.
  4. A log entry does not keep the namespace it was told in, so a replay qualifies it again: `w.tell('@e.user agent', { ns: '' })` on a world with `ns: 'A'`, then `_replay(w.log)` on a second world with `ns: 'A'` gives `A::e.user` (the original holds `e.user`). `World.restore`/`_replay` should store `ns` per entry. (The host rewrites the entry to `::e.user` while it replays: `replayInto`.)
  5. A reference without a namespace to a fact of a `shared=true` type that exists only in the default namespace does not resolve ("references local first" stops at the namespace): `@agent core.type shared=true`, `w.tell('@e.user agent', { ns: '' })`, `w.tell('@p1 pet owner=$e.user name=rex')` in `ns: 'A'` is `dangling-ref $A::e.user`; `owner=$::e.user` works. It should fall back to the default namespace for shared types. (The host writes `$::e.user`: `toLib`.)
  6. Untested but read in the source: `check(patch, { query })` runs the inner query with `{}` (no `ns`), so a `where` reference in it is not qualified.
- **Host findings from the warnings.** `unknown-attribute template.item` on `tasks.a.add` (the host reads `template.<key>`, expected) and `card=many` on `gs.k.lt` / `gs.k.lx` of `games.guess` (the library reads `many=true`; the slots are therefore single-valued today; left as it is so that behaviour stays identical; worth a decision).
- **Not done / honest limits.** `shared=true` for `core.agent` has no effect until item 3 (the instances are single-namespace, so nothing depends on it); `docs.editor` is the one instance in the default namespace (item 2); there is no per-turn namespace: facts of different turns must join (a count over purchases, a fluent over events), and a namespace per turn broke every aggregate and every reference to an agent in a trial (answers: "I don't know how much money you have"); the turn goes in the ids instead.

## REGRESSION-AB (2026-11-12): matrix no longer worse than ops in the dialogues

- **Result.** `ab-sopworld --sets coherence,dialogues,gold` (803 sessions, `training/results/sopworld-ab-2026-11-12.json`, classes in `.classes.json`): 0 "ops better" (7 before), 11 matrix better, 15 neutral wording, 0 unjudged (the 8 unjudged dialogues and one gold session are classified with notes). One open question in the classes file: dlg.avt#011, an undo of "Yes" (become the emoji) goes back to the library state before it, which still holds the open offer (`ui.avatar.pending`); ops forgets the offer.
- **Chess (gms#002, gms#006, doc#035): a real bug, fixed.** Two causes. (1) The engine's late reply ("Meanwhile I play knight to c6.") was told inside the user's next turn, so one timeline step held both plies. A world now says it with `ctx.checkpoint(text)` (ops) or `live.checkpoint(text)` (fns): the host adopts what the world has so far and begins the user's step after it (`ReasonerSession.checkpoint`); an action cannot mark inside its transaction (the library throws "committed to a state shared with a fork"), so the fn commits, stops, and `resolve` marks and runs it again with `live.carry` as the first words. (2) "Make your move." (the wait counter) was a step of its own, so the engine's reply of the next turn was rewound with it and the undo stopped one ply early. `adopt` may now return `quiet: true` (games.chess: only the transient rest moved): no step. Engine replies that arrive as observations were already outside every turn. (3) doc#035: a generic undo did not note its turn for "what did you do?"; `discourse.noteWorld` now does, as the world's own undo does.
- **The four "same reply, worse outcome": a view difference, not a told fact (decision (b)).** kidsA#010: "Zoe eats 8. How many marbles are left?" is half understood; "Zoe ate 8 marbles" is told in BOTH modes (turn 3 says 11 in both), matrix only shows it in `kids.arith.holdings` (spec 15 `toState`); ops keeps it in wires. The judge compared the whole state, so the not-understood part was "wrong" in matrix, "not understood" in ops. drm#011/014/021: art.draw's matrix view had no `history`/`future` and a refused turn made the empty lists appear (the ops operations create them), so the key set changed on a turn that changed nothing. Fix: art.draw's `toState` keeps `history: []`, `future: []` (the view no longer changes shape), and the judges (`scripts/lib/visible-state.mjs`, used by ab-sopworld and the eval-* scripts) compare the state without `ui.help` and `kids.arith.holdings`. Nothing is told on a not-understood turn in either mode.
- **Gate.** `tests/ab-gate.test.mjs` runs `ab-sopworld --sets dialogues --ids <17 dialogues, the seven among them> --no-write` and fails on any "ops better" (6 s). `tests/regression-ab.test.mjs`: the three chess dialogues in both modes, a second undo, and the observation path. Chrome: `chess-late` block in `scripts/test-webchat-matrix.mjs` (race-tolerant rows).
- **Gates run.** `npm test`, `test-world all` (both reasoners), `build-webchat.mjs`, `test-webchat-matrix.mjs` 186/186, `test:browser` (see the report).

## RO-LEFTOVERS (2026-11-12): the four leftovers of RO-TRANSLATE-FALLBACK

- **1. English word inside a Romanian sentence.** `englishLeftovers` now also catches the regular forms of every word of the English lexicon (`biggest`, `smaller`, `running`, plural, `-ly`), not only the listed words: "Perele sunt biggest, cu 5." is no longer accepted from the on-device Translator. Guards: Romanian morphology, names, numbers, quoted text. `tests/ro-leftovers.test.mjs` tests both ways (leaks found; canonical Romanian sentences and the Romanian operational messages not flagged beyond a tiny known list: link, stop, show, state, ...).

## 2026-11-12 geomap intermittent (map tab echoing its view)
- Cause: docs/webchat/ui/tabs/map.mjs reported its view (zoom/center) to the world 350 ms after every `moveend`, including the moves the world itself asked for (setMaxZoom clamp on the offline layer, animated flyTo). `host.setWorldState` puts that into the session state and the library `adopt`s it at the next operation as a user change (a turn that clears the redo steps, a new undo step): a fractional intermediate zoom (14.75) reached the state, and a late report landed between undo and redo so that redo did not restore the offline layer.
- Fix (product): a guard (`programmatic`) suppresses reports while apply() changes the layer or flies; apply() cancels a pending report; and a report equal to the state the world set (zoom, center, no bounds) is dropped as an echo. A clamp, undo and redo of the library now win. No unit test: the race is in the Leaflet tab (browser only); the Node side (adopt of a host patch) is by design.
- Other tabs: data.charts, art.draw, diagram.sketch do not call host.setWorldState (they only send commands), so they have no echo path; docs.editor (selection/cursor on user click only), docs.reader (visible flag, asserted once), media.music (playing=false on end) report on user events only.
- Gates: geomap 14/14 under 14 parallel throttled Chrome runs (before: about 1 in 12 failed), full matrix and npm test, see commit.

## 2026-11-12 MINIMAL-PAIRS and COMMON-SENSE (worker)

**MINIMAL-PAIRS.**
- `training/eval/minimal-pairs-2026-11-12.jsonl`: 122 cases (the reviewer's 22 + 100 new), every case with its swapped twin (`twin`), over kids.arith (45), kids.shop (15), art.draw (20), geo.map (14), games.chess (14), docs.editor (14). Chess positions are reached by playing (the setup list; the built-in engine is deterministic and answers at the next rejected message), because `setWorldState` of a chess FEN does not reach the library instance (chess is owned by the matrix, the state is only adopted from operations). Negation cases forbid "did not understand" in the answer: understood and said back is the requirement.
- Done: kids.arith `kids.arith.relation` (more / fewer / times as many; "twice", "half", "3 times"), both reasoners (ops `replay`, matrix `arith.r.rl.*`); `kids.arith.negated` and `kids.shop.negated` (understood, "Nothing changes."); kids.shop "I give Ana 10 lei" (expense). Words are `lang.common` groups `lang.cmp.*`, `lang.neg.aux`, `lang.neg.any`, `lang.neg.present` (shop uses the present-only group: "I didn't buy milk" belongs to kids.tasks). Patterns are hand-written with these groups (descriptions are off by default, so a description would not have run).
- Right / wrong / not understood (rules mode, judge with the answer-part fix), before -> after: kids.arith 29/4/12 -> 45/0/0 (of 45); kids.shop 8/7/0 -> 15/0/0; art.draw 18/0/2 -> 18/0/2; geo.map 10/0/2 (plus 2 partly) -> same; games.chess 14/0/0 -> same; docs.editor 10/2/2 -> same (right / wrong / not understood; the 22-case set the reviewer ran was 16 right, 1 wrong, 5 not understood). Total 89 -> 112 right of 122 (73.0% -> 91.8%). Operational set (rules) unchanged: 95.4% right, 0.7% wrong.
- Still fails, and why: art.draw "make the cat bigger than the square" (a relative-size command does not exist, "Did you mean"; 2 cases); geo.map "mark Paris and then Rome" (the elided verb is read as "show Rome": the second marker is not made; 2 partly) and "go from Berlin to Vienna" (no such command word; 2); docs.editor "move chapter 2 before chapter 3" (the draw-style move is chosen, "Where should it go?": the editor has no move-before/after block command; 2) and "swap the first and the second paragraph" (not understood, and the rest of the message does the wrong thing: "went to the second paragraph"; 2 wrong). Left for the editor/map owners; not patched with ad-hoc patterns.
- Known limit: a comparison fixes the pair when it is told; events after it are not carried to the other person. A comparison that would make a count negative is refused as a conflict (ops) / soft issue (matrix).

**COMMON-SENSE.**
- New world `worlds/common.sense` (id `world.common.sense`, namespace `common.sense`, status=fixture): types, facts (`kb.sop`: `livesin`, `rule`, `place`, `single`, `absent`) and three soft rules (`cs.d.place`, `cs.d.single`, `cs.d.night`), no patterns, no operations, no host logic. art.draw declares `compose=world.common.sense` (and imports the legend); nothing is copied. Spec 15 section "Common sense, advice and defaults".
- Host: `ReasonerSession.adoptState` keeps the state when the only new issues are advice (`cs.d.*`), `takeAdvice()`, and `runAction` appends them in brackets after the action's answer, once while the issue stands; a hard issue is still a refusal. art.draw `fns.mjs` tells `common.sense.thing / at / moment` in `adopt`; `ops.mjs` reads the `usual=yes` rows for defaults and no longer has an anchor table (`scene.mjs` lost `anchor:` of every kind). Fish are `must=yes`: a pond is made (the sea when the picture has no ground and nothing standing on it); boats use water when there is some.
- Intended changes to existing tests (all in art.draw/tests.sop, mechanical): a fish in a picture that has a house or tree now brings its pond, one more object before the fish: `parts06..08`, `r2add02..r2add34` (object counts and indexes +1 from the first fish on) and nothing else; no test text changed. tests/ro-rp5.test.mjs RP5b uses a cat instead of a fish ("a fish makes its water itself"); tests/reasoner.test.mjs lists common.sense and the new test counts (kids.shop 167, kids.arith 243, art.draw 571). The four warning tests carry `reasoner=matrix`.
- New: tests/commonsense.test.mjs, 9 art.draw tests (`cs01..cs09`), Chrome scenario `commonsense` in scripts/test-webchat-draw-matrix.mjs (fish in the sky, a second sun, a car in the sky: warned once, never refused; a plain fish gets its water with no warning).
- Gotcha: soplang v1 parity test: only matrix/mapping files may carry `#` comments; the other .sop files may not.
- Limit: advice and place notes ("I made a pond for the fish.") are English strings appended to the action's answer; in a Romanian session the whole answer falls to the translator fallback, so Romanian users get the English sentence until MT-SERIOUS lands (RO tests that drew fish/moved things into water use a cat).
- Library items (sopworld): (1) **A world composed into several composites.** `compose=world.common.sense` in art.draw and in, say, geo.map: the second declaration is ignored (`homes` keeps the first, a composed world has no instance of its own), so common sense lives in exactly one host instance. Repro: add `compose=world.common.sense` to geo.map/world.sop and ask `reasoner.instKey('common.sense')` (it stays art.draw); wanted: one facts world read by several instances (a shared read-only world, `reads=` that also brings the matrix rules). (2) **A soft deny with an everyday reason per row.** `why=` takes `?var`s of the matched wires, so the reason is a fact joined in the rule (`rule.say`, `place.phrase`); wanted: `why=` from a field of a joined fact without writing the join, and an issue attribute (`advice=true`) so the host does not have to recognise it by the rule id prefix `cs.d.`. (3) **Issue per ordered pair.** `cs.d.single` on two things of one kind makes two issues ((a,b) and (b,a)); the host dedupes the text. Wanted: an unordered `when` pair, or `core.check fn=lt` on ids.
- Gates: `npm test` (see commit), `test-world all` both reasoners, webchat built, Chrome matrix.

## 2026-11-13 SPEED-INDEPENDENT READINGS, SMOKE and FULL suites (worker)
- **Load-sensitive name repair (finding 1).** Causes: `nameCandidates` had `budgetMs: 30` (wall clock; a busy box stopped the window scan before "Pariss"), the dialog added `NAME_BUDGET_MS = 120`, the leniency stage had `LIMITS.lenientMs` (3 s `performance.now` deadline) and `guard.soft()` compared the clock with the soft deadline (stopped partial windows, best effort, the hybrid refine and the validation loops). Now: names use `maxSteps` 4000 (windows and edit distances), leniency `LENIENT_MAX_STEPS` 600 checks, `guard.soft()` is true only when the turn was stopped or hit the hard deadline (`softMs` accepted, ignored); `lenientMs` removed from the time limits. Test: rd10 "the reading does not depend on the clock" (clock frozen, 50 ms and 5 s per call, same reading for Pariss / New Youk / Londn). Specs 03 and 06 updated.
- **Other clock uses found** (not converted, reasons): hard turn deadline (`guard.check`, aborts visibly, the intended safety stop); per-operation cap `opTimeoutMs` in the interpreter (a visible "needed more effort" answer, deterministic operations; with a 50 ms-per-call clock every operation answers that, which is why the test compares readings, not answers); model calls with deadlines (`ort-client` deadlines scaled by the device speed factor, `normalizer-runtime` deadline, `strategy.mjs` encoder timeout 1500 ms, dispatcher/normalizer `time-limits`): inference time, they degrade to the rules answer, so a slow device can read differently with a model on (open: the rules-only reading is speed independent); `precompile` yielding (no effect on a reading), perf timing, restart window, `knowledge-lookup`/`facts` timing stats.
